                "content": "# WebGLRenderer\n\nThis renderer uses WebGL 2 to display scenes.\n\nWebGL 1 is not supported since `r163`.\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new WebGLRenderer( parameters : WebGLRenderer~Options )\n\nConstructs a new WebGL renderer.\n\n**parameters**\n\nThe configuration parameter.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .autoClear : boolean\n\nWhether the renderer should automatically clear its output before rendering a frame or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .autoClearColor : boolean\n\nIf [WebGLRenderer#autoClear](WebGLRenderer.html#autoClear) set to `true`, whether the renderer should clear the color buffer or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .autoClearDepth : boolean\n\nIf [WebGLRenderer#autoClear](WebGLRenderer.html#autoClear) set to `true`, whether the renderer should clear the depth buffer or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .autoClearStencil : boolean\n\nIf [WebGLRenderer#autoClear](WebGLRenderer.html#autoClear) set to `true`, whether the renderer should clear the stencil buffer or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .capabilities : WebGLRenderer~Capabilities\n\nHolds details about the capabilities of the current rendering context.\n\n### .clippingPlanes : Array.<Plane>\n\nUser-defined clipping planes specified in world space. These planes apply globally. Points in space whose dot product with the plane is negative are cut away.\n\n### .coordinateSystem : WebGLCoordinateSystem | WebGPUCoordinateSystem (readonly)\n\nDefines the coordinate system of the renderer.\n\nIn `WebGLRenderer`, the value is always `WebGLCoordinateSystem`.\n\nDefault is `WebGLCoordinateSystem`.\n\n### .debug : Object\n\nA object with debug configuration settings.\n\n*   `checkShaderErrors`: If it is `true`, defines whether material shader programs are checked for errors during compilation and linkage process. It may be useful to disable this check in production for performance gain. It is strongly recommended to keep these checks enabled during development. If the shader does not compile and link, it will not work and associated material will not render.\n*   `onShaderError(gl, program, glVertexShader,glFragmentShader)`: A callback function that can be used for custom error reporting. The callback receives the WebGL context, an instance of WebGLProgram as well two instances of WebGLShader representing the vertex and fragment shader. Assigning a custom function disables the default error reporting.\n\n### .domElement : HTMLCanvasElement | OffscreenCanvas\n\nA canvas where the renderer draws its output. This is automatically created by the renderer in the constructor (if not provided already); you just need to add it to your page like so:\n\n```js\ndocument.body.appendChild( renderer.domElement );\n```\n\n### .extensions : Object\n\nProvides methods for retrieving and testing WebGL extensions.\n\n*   `get(extensionName:string)`: Used to check whether a WebGL extension is supported and return the extension object if available.\n*   `has(extensionName:string)`: returns `true` if the extension is supported.\n\n### .info : WebGLRenderer~Info\n\nHolds a series of statistical information about the GPU memory and the rendering process. Useful for debugging and monitoring.\n\nBy default these data are reset at each render call but when having multiple render passes per frame (e.g. when using post processing) it can be preferred to reset with a custom pattern. First, set `autoReset` to `false`.\n\n```js\nrenderer.info.autoReset = false;\n```\n\nCall `reset()` whenever you have finished to render a single frame.\n\n```js\nrenderer.info.reset();\n```\n\n### .isWebGLRenderer : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .localClippingEnabled : boolean\n\nWhether the renderer respects object-level clipping planes or not.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .outputColorSpace : SRGBColorSpace | LinearSRGBColorSpace\n\nDefines the output color space of the renderer.\n\nDefault is `SRGBColorSpace`.\n\n### .properties : Object\n\nUsed to track properties of other objects like native WebGL objects.\n\n### .renderLists : Object\n\nManages the render lists of the renderer.\n\n### .shadowMap : WebGLRenderer~ShadowMap\n\nInterface for managing shadows.\n\n### .sortObjects : boolean\n\nWhether the renderer should sort objects or not.\n\nNote: Sorting is used to attempt to properly render objects that have some degree of transparency. By definition, sorting objects may not work in all cases. Depending on the needs of application, it may be necessary to turn off sorting and use other methods to deal with transparency rendering e.g. manually determining each object's rendering order.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .state : Object\n\nInterface for managing the WebGL state.\n\n### .toneMapping : NoToneMapping | LinearToneMapping | ReinhardToneMapping | CineonToneMapping | ACESFilmicToneMapping | CustomToneMapping | AgXToneMapping | NeutralToneMapping\n\nThe tone mapping technique of the renderer.\n\nDefault is `NoToneMapping`.\n\n### .toneMappingExposure : number\n\nExposure level of tone mapping.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .transmissionResolutionScale : number\n\nThe normalized resolution scale for the transmission render target, measured in percentage of viewport dimensions. Lowering this value can result in significant performance improvements when using [MeshPhysicalMaterial#transmission](MeshPhysicalMaterial.html#transmission).\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .xr : WebXRManager\n\nA reference to the XR manager.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .clear( color : boolean, depth : boolean, stencil : boolean )\n\nTells the renderer to clear its color, depth or stencil drawing buffer(s). This method initializes the buffers to the current clear color values.\n\n**color**\n\nWhether the color buffer should be cleared or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n**depth**\n\nWhether the depth buffer should be cleared or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n**stencil**\n\nWhether the stencil buffer should be cleared or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .clearColor()\n\nClears the color buffer. Equivalent to calling `renderer.clear( true, false, false )`.\n\n### .clearDepth()\n\nClears the depth buffer. Equivalent to calling `renderer.clear( false, true, false )`.\n\n### .clearStencil()\n\nClears the stencil buffer. Equivalent to calling `renderer.clear( false, false, true )`.\n\n### .compile( scene : Object3D, camera : Camera, targetScene : Scene ) : Set.<Material>\n\nCompiles all materials in the scene with the camera. This is useful to precompile shaders before the first rendering. If you want to add a 3D object to an existing scene, use the third optional parameter for applying the target scene.\n\nNote that the (target) scene's lighting and environment must be configured before calling this method.\n\n**scene**\n\nThe scene or another type of 3D object to precompile.\n\n**camera**\n\nThe camera.\n\n**targetScene**\n\nThe target scene.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n**Returns:** The precompiled materials.\n\n### .compileAsync( scene : Object3D, camera : Camera, targetScene : Scene ) : Promise (async)\n\nAsynchronous version of [WebGLRenderer#compile](WebGLRenderer.html#compile).\n\nThis method makes use of the `KHR_parallel_shader_compile` WebGL extension. Hence, it is recommended to use this version of `compile()` whenever possible.\n\n**scene**\n\nThe scene or another type of 3D object to precompile.\n\n**camera**\n\nThe camera.\n\n**targetScene**\n\nThe target scene.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n**Returns:** A Promise that resolves when the given scene can be rendered without unnecessary stalling due to shader compilation.\n\n### .copyFramebufferToTexture( texture : FramebufferTexture, position : Vector2, level : number )\n\nCopies pixels from the current bound framebuffer into the given texture.\n\n**texture**\n\nThe texture.\n\n**position**\n\nThe start position of the copy operation.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n**level**\n\nThe mip level.                 "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher → Object3D →*\n\n# Scene\n\nScenes allow you to set up what is to be rendered and where by three.js. This is where you place 3D objects like meshes, lines or lights.\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new Scene()\n\nConstructs a new scene.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .background : Color | Texture\n\nDefines the background of the scene. Valid inputs are:\n\n*   A color for defining a uniform colored background.\n*   A texture for defining a (flat) textured background.\n*   Cube textures or equirectangular textures for defining a skybox.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .backgroundBlurriness : number\n\nSets the blurriness of the background. Only influences environment maps assigned to [Scene#background](Scene.html#background). Valid input is a float between `0` and `1`.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .backgroundIntensity : number\n\nAttenuates the color of the background. Only applies to background textures.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .backgroundRotation : Euler\n\nThe rotation of the background in radians. Only influences environment maps assigned to [Scene#background](Scene.html#background).\n\nDefault is `(0,0,0)`.\n\n### .environment : Texture\n\nSets the environment map for all physical materials in the scene. However, it's not possible to overwrite an existing texture assigned to the `envMap` material property.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .environmentIntensity : number\n\nAttenuates the color of the environment. Only influences environment maps assigned to [Scene#environment](Scene.html#environment).\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .environmentRotation : Euler\n\nThe rotation of the environment map in radians. Only influences physical materials in the scene when [Scene#environment](Scene.html#environment) is used.\n\nDefault is `(0,0,0)`.\n\n### .fog : Fog | FogExp2\n\nA fog instance defining the type of fog that affects everything rendered in the scene.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .isScene : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .overrideMaterial : Material\n\nForces everything in the scene to be rendered with the defined material. It is possible to exclude materials from override by setting [Material#allowOverride](Material.html#allowOverride) to `false`.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n## Source\n\n[src/scenes/Scene.js](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/master/src/scenes/Scene.js)"

                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher → Object3D → Camera →*\n\n# PerspectiveCamera\n\nCamera that uses [perspective projection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perspective_\\(graphical\\)).\n\nThis projection mode is designed to mimic the way the human eye sees. It is the most common projection mode used for rendering a 3D scene.\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\nconst camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera( 45, width / height, 1, 1000 );\nscene.add( camera );\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new PerspectiveCamera( fov : number, aspect : number, near : number, far : number )\n\nConstructs a new perspective camera.\n\n**fov**\n\nThe vertical field of view.\n\nDefault is `50`.\n\n**aspect**\n\nThe aspect ratio.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n**near**\n\nThe camera's near plane.\n\nDefault is `0.1`.\n\n**far**\n\nThe camera's far plane.\n\nDefault is `2000`.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .aspect : number\n\nThe aspect ratio, usually the canvas width / canvas height.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .far : number\n\nThe camera's far plane. Must be greater than the current value of [PerspectiveCamera#near](PerspectiveCamera.html#near).\n\nDefault is `2000`.\n\n### .filmGauge : number\n\nFilm size used for the larger axis. Default is `35` (millimeters). This parameter does not influence the projection matrix unless [PerspectiveCamera#filmOffset](PerspectiveCamera.html#filmOffset) is set to a nonzero value.\n\nDefault is `35`.\n\n### .filmOffset : number\n\nHorizontal off-center offset in the same unit as [PerspectiveCamera#filmGauge](PerspectiveCamera.html#filmGauge).\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .focus : number\n\nObject distance used for stereoscopy and depth-of-field effects. This parameter does not influence the projection matrix unless a [StereoCamera](StereoCamera.html) is being used.\n\nDefault is `10`.\n\n### .fov : number\n\nThe vertical field of view, from bottom to top of view, in degrees.\n\nDefault is `50`.\n\n### .isPerspectiveCamera : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .near : number\n\nThe camera's near plane. The valid range is greater than `0` and less than the current value of [PerspectiveCamera#far](PerspectiveCamera.html#far).\n\nNote that, unlike for the [OrthographicCamera](OrthographicCamera.html), `0` is _not_ a valid value for a perspective camera's near plane.\n\nDefault is `0.1`.\n\n### .view : Object\n\nRepresents the frustum window specification. This property should not be edited directly but via [PerspectiveCamera#setViewOffset](PerspectiveCamera.html#setViewOffset) and [PerspectiveCamera#clearViewOffset](PerspectiveCamera.html#clearViewOffset).\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .zoom : number\n\nThe zoom factor of the camera.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .clearViewOffset()\n\nRemoves the view offset from the projection matrix.\n\n### .getEffectiveFOV() : number\n\nReturns the current vertical field of view angle in degrees considering [PerspectiveCamera#zoom](PerspectiveCamera.html#zoom).\n\n**Returns:** The effective FOV.\n\n### .getFilmHeight() : number\n\nReturns the height of the image on the film. If [PerspectiveCamera#aspect](PerspectiveCamera.html#aspect) is greater than or equal to one (landscape format), the result equals [PerspectiveCamera#filmGauge](PerspectiveCamera.html#filmGauge).\n\n**Returns:** The film width.\n\n### .getFilmWidth() : number\n\nReturns the width of the image on the film. If [PerspectiveCamera#aspect](PerspectiveCamera.html#aspect) is greater than or equal to one (landscape format), the result equals [PerspectiveCamera#filmGauge](PerspectiveCamera.html#filmGauge).\n\n**Returns:** The film width.\n\n### .getFocalLength() : number\n\nReturns the focal length from the current [PerspectiveCamera#fov](PerspectiveCamera.html#fov) and [PerspectiveCamera#filmGauge](PerspectiveCamera.html#filmGauge).\n\n**Returns:** The computed focal length.\n\n### .getViewBounds( distance : number, minTarget : Vector2, maxTarget : Vector2 )\n\nComputes the 2D bounds of the camera's viewable rectangle at a given distance along the viewing direction. Sets `minTarget` and `maxTarget` to the coordinates of the lower-left and upper-right corners of the view rectangle.\n\n**distance**\n\nThe viewing distance.\n\n**minTarget**\n\nThe lower-left corner of the view rectangle is written into this vector.\n\n**maxTarget**\n\nThe upper-right corner of the view rectangle is written into this vector.\n\n### .getViewSize( distance : number, target : Vector2 ) : Vector2\n\nComputes the width and height of the camera's viewable rectangle at a given distance along the viewing direction.\n\n**distance**\n\nThe viewing distance.\n\n**target**\n\nThe target vector that is used to store result where x is width and y is height.\n\n**Returns:** The view size.\n\n### .setFocalLength( focalLength : number )\n\nSets the FOV by focal length in respect to the current [PerspectiveCamera#filmGauge](PerspectiveCamera.html#filmGauge).\n\nThe default film gauge is 35, so that the focal length can be specified for a 35mm (full frame) camera.\n\n**focalLength**\n\nValues for focal length and film gauge must have the same unit.\n\n### .setViewOffset( fullWidth : number, fullHeight : number, x : number, y : number, width : number, height : number )\n\nSets an offset in a larger frustum. This is useful for multi-window or multi-monitor/multi-machine setups.\n\nFor example, if you have 3x2 monitors and each monitor is 1920x1080 and the monitors are in grid like this\n\n```js\n+---+---+---+\n  | A | B | C |\n  +---+---+---+\n  | D | E | F |\n  +---+---+---+\n```\n\nthen for each monitor you would call it like this:\n\n```js\nconst w = 1920;\nconst h = 1080;\nconst fullWidth = w * 3;\nconst fullHeight = h * 2;\n// --A--\ncamera.setViewOffset( fullWidth, fullHeight, w * 0, h * 0, w, h );\n// --B--\ncamera.setViewOffset( fullWidth, fullHeight, w * 1, h * 0, w, h );\n// --C--\ncamera.setViewOffset( fullWidth, fullHeight, w * 2, h * 0, w, h );\n// --D--\ncamera.setViewOffset( fullWidth, fullHeight, w * 0, h * 1, w, h );\n// --E--\ncamera.setViewOffset( fullWidth, fullHeight, w * 1, h * 1, w, h );\n// --F--\ncamera.setViewOffset( fullWidth, fullHeight, w * 2, h * 1, w, h );\n```\n\nNote there is no reason monitors have to be the same size or in a grid.\n\n**fullWidth**\n\nThe full width of multiview setup.\n\n**fullHeight**\n\nThe full height of multiview setup.\n\n**x**\n\nThe horizontal offset of the subcamera.\n\n**y**\n\nThe vertical offset of the subcamera.\n\n**width**\n\nThe width of subcamera.\n\n**height**\n\nThe height of subcamera.\n\n### .updateProjectionMatrix()\n\nUpdates the camera's projection matrix. Must be called after any change of camera properties.\n\n## Source\n\n[src/cameras/PerspectiveCamera.js](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/master/src/cameras/PerspectiveCamera.js)"

                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher → Object3D →*\n\n# Mesh\n\nClass representing triangular polygon mesh based objects.\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\nconst geometry = new THREE.BoxGeometry( 1, 1, 1 );\nconst material = new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial( { color: 0xffff00 } );\nconst mesh = new THREE.Mesh( geometry, material );\nscene.add( mesh );\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new Mesh( geometry : BufferGeometry, material : Material | Array.<Material> )\n\nConstructs a new mesh.\n\n**geometry**\n\nThe mesh geometry.\n\n**material**\n\nThe mesh material.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .count : number\n\nThe number of instances of this mesh. Can only be used with [WebGPURenderer](WebGPURenderer.html).\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .geometry : BufferGeometry\n\nThe mesh geometry.\n\n### .isMesh : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .material : Material | Array.<Material>\n\nThe mesh material.\n\nDefault is `MeshBasicMaterial`.\n\n### .morphTargetDictionary : Object.<string, number> | undefined\n\nA dictionary representing the morph targets in the geometry. The key is the morph targets name, the value its attribute index. This member is `undefined` by default and only set when morph targets are detected in the geometry.\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n### .morphTargetInfluences : Array.<number> | undefined\n\nAn array of weights typically in the range `[0,1]` that specify how much of the morph is applied. This member is `undefined` by default and only set when morph targets are detected in the geometry.\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .getVertexPosition( index : number, target : Vector3 ) : Vector3\n\nReturns the local-space position of the vertex at the given index, taking into account the current animation state of both morph targets and skinning.\n\n**index**\n\nThe vertex index.\n\n**target**\n\nThe target object that is used to store the method's result.\n\n**Returns:** The vertex position in local space.\n\n### .raycast( raycaster : Raycaster, intersects : Array.<Object> )\n\nComputes intersection points between a casted ray and this line.\n\n**raycaster**\n\nThe raycaster.\n\n**intersects**\n\nThe target array that holds the intersection points.\n\n**Overrides:** [Object3D#raycast](Object3D.html#raycast)\n\n### .updateMorphTargets()\n\nSets the values of [Mesh#morphTargetDictionary](Mesh.html#morphTargetDictionary) and [Mesh#morphTargetInfluences](Mesh.html#morphTargetInfluences) to make sure existing morph targets can influence this 3D object.\n\n## Source\n\n[src/objects/Mesh.js](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/master/src/objects/Mesh.js)"

                "name": "MeshStandardMaterial",
                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher → Material →*\n\n# MeshStandardMaterial\n\nA standard physically based material, using Metallic-Roughness workflow.\n\nPhysically based rendering (PBR) has recently become the standard in many 3D applications, such as [Unity](https://blogs.unity3d.com/2014/10/29/physically-based-shading-in-unity-5-a-primer/), [Unreal](https://docs.unrealengine.com/latest/INT/Engine/Rendering/Materials/PhysicallyBased/) and [3D Studio Max](http://area.autodesk.com/blogs/the-3ds-max-blog/what039s-new-for-rendering-in-3ds-max-2017).\n\nThis approach differs from older approaches in that instead of using approximations for the way in which light interacts with a surface, a physically correct model is used. The idea is that, instead of tweaking materials to look good under specific lighting, a material can be created that will react 'correctly' under all lighting scenarios.\n\nIn practice this gives a more accurate and realistic looking result than the [MeshLambertMaterial](MeshLambertMaterial.html) or [MeshPhongMaterial](MeshPhongMaterial.html), at the cost of being somewhat more computationally expensive. `MeshStandardMaterial` uses per-fragment shading.\n\nNote that for best results you should always specify an environment map when using this material.\n\nFor a non-technical introduction to the concept of PBR and how to set up a PBR material, check out these articles by the people at [marmoset](https://www.marmoset.co):\n\n*   [Basic Theory of Physically Based Rendering](https://www.marmoset.co/posts/basic-theory-of-physically-based-rendering/)\n*   [Physically Based Rendering and You Can Too](https://www.marmoset.co/posts/physically-based-rendering-and-you-can-too/)\n\nTechnical details of the approach used in three.js (and most other PBR systems) can be found is this [paper from Disney](https://media.disneyanimation.com/uploads/production/publication_asset/48/asset/s2012_pbs_disney_brdf_notes_v3.pdf) (pdf), by Brent Burley.\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new MeshStandardMaterial( parameters : Object )\n\nConstructs a new mesh standard material.\n\n**parameters**\n\nAn object with one or more properties defining the material's appearance. Any property of the material (including any property from inherited materials) can be passed in here. Color values can be passed any type of value accepted by [Color#set](Color.html#set).\n\n## Properties\n\n### .alphaMap : Texture\n\nThe alpha map is a grayscale texture that controls the opacity across the surface (black: fully transparent; white: fully opaque).\n\nOnly the color of the texture is used, ignoring the alpha channel if one exists. For RGB and RGBA textures, the renderer will use the green channel when sampling this texture due to the extra bit of precision provided for green in DXT-compressed and uncompressed RGB 565 formats. Luminance-only and luminance/alpha textures will also still work as expected.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .aoMap : Texture\n\nThe red channel of this texture is used as the ambient occlusion map. Requires a second set of UVs.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .aoMapIntensity : number\n\nIntensity of the ambient occlusion effect. Range is `[0,1]`, where `0` disables ambient occlusion. Where intensity is `1` and the AO map's red channel is also `1`, ambient light is fully occluded on a surface.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .bumpMap : Texture\n\nThe texture to create a bump map. The black and white values map to the perceived depth in relation to the lights. Bump doesn't actually affect the geometry of the object, only the lighting. If a normal map is defined this will be ignored.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .bumpScale : number\n\nHow much the bump map affects the material. Typical range is `[0,1]`.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .color : Color\n\nColor of the material.\n\nDefault is `(1,1,1)`.\n\n### .displacementBias : number\n\nThe offset of the displacement map's values on the mesh's vertices. The bias is added to the scaled sample of the displacement map. Without a displacement map set, this value is not applied.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .displacementMap : Texture\n\nThe displacement map affects the position of the mesh's vertices. Unlike other maps which only affect the light and shade of the material the displaced vertices can cast shadows, block other objects, and otherwise act as real geometry. The displacement texture is an image where the value of each pixel (white being the highest) is mapped against, and repositions, the vertices of the mesh.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .displacementScale : number\n\nHow much the displacement map affects the mesh (where black is no displacement, and white is maximum displacement). Without a displacement map set, this value is not applied.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .emissive : Color\n\nEmissive (light) color of the material, essentially a solid color unaffected by other lighting.\n\nDefault is `(0,0,0)`.\n\n### .emissiveIntensity : number\n\nIntensity of the emissive light. Modulates the emissive color.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .emissiveMap : Texture\n\nSet emissive (glow) map. The emissive map color is modulated by the emissive color and the emissive intensity. If you have an emissive map, be sure to set the emissive color to something other than black.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .envMap : Texture\n\nThe environment map. To ensure a physically correct rendering, environment maps are internally pre-processed with [PMREMGenerator](PMREMGenerator.html).\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .envMapIntensity : number\n\nScales the effect of the environment map by multiplying its color.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .envMapRotation : Euler\n\nThe rotation of the environment map in radians.\n\nDefault is `(0,0,0)`.\n\n### .flatShading : boolean\n\nWhether the material is rendered with flat shading or not.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .fog : boolean\n\nWhether the material is affected by fog or not.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .isMeshStandardMaterial : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .lightMap : Texture\n\nThe light map. Requires a second set of UVs.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .lightMapIntensity : number\n\nIntensity of the baked light.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .map : Texture\n\nThe color map. May optionally include an alpha channel, typically combined with [Material#transparent](Material.html#transparent) or [Material#alphaTest](Material.html#alphaTest). The texture map color is modulated by the diffuse `color`.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .metalness : number\n\nHow much the material is like a metal. Non-metallic materials such as wood or stone use `0.0`, metallic use `1.0`, with nothing (usually) in between. A value between `0.0` and `1.0` could be used for a rusty metal look. If `metalnessMap` is also provided, both values are multiplied.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .metalnessMap : Texture\n\nThe blue channel of this texture is used to alter the metalness of the material.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .normalMap : Texture\n\nThe texture to create a normal map. The RGB values affect the surface normal for each pixel fragment and change the way the color is lit. Normal maps do not change the actual shape of the surface, only the lighting. In case the material has a normal map authored using the left handed convention, the `y` component of `normalScale` should be negated to compensate for the different handedness.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .normalMapType : TangentSpaceNormalMap | ObjectSpaceNormalMap\n\nThe type of normal map.\n\nDefault is `TangentSpaceNormalMap`.\n\n### .normalScale : Vector2\n\nHow much the normal map affects the material. Typical value range is `[0,1]`.\n\nDefault is `(1,1)`.\n\n### .roughness : number\n\nHow rough the material appears. `0.0` means a smooth mirror reflection, `1.0` means fully diffuse. If `roughnessMap` is also provided, both values are multiplied.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .roughnessMap : Texture\n\nThe green channel of this texture is used to alter the roughness of the material.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .wireframe : boolean\n\nRenders the geometry as a wireframe.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .wireframeLinecap : 'round' | 'bevel' | 'miter'\n\nDefines app"
            },

                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher → Object3D → Light →*\n\n# DirectionalLight\n\nA light that gets emitted in a specific direction. This light will behave as though it is infinitely far away and the rays produced from it are all parallel. The common use case for this is to simulate daylight; the sun is far enough away that its position can be considered to be infinite, and all light rays coming from it are parallel.\n\nA common point of confusion for directional lights is that setting the rotation has no effect. This is because three.js's DirectionalLight is the equivalent to what is often called a 'Target Direct Light' in other applications.\n\nThis means that its direction is calculated as pointing from the light's [Object3D#position](Object3D.html#position) to the [DirectionalLight#target](DirectionalLight.html#target) position (as opposed to a 'Free Direct Light' that just has a rotation component).\n\nThis light can cast shadows - see the [DirectionalLightShadow](DirectionalLightShadow.html) for details.\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\n// White directional light at half intensity shining from the top.\nconst directionalLight = new THREE.DirectionalLight( 0xffffff, 0.5 );\nscene.add( directionalLight );\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new DirectionalLight( color : number | Color | string, intensity : number )\n\nConstructs a new directional light.\n\n**color**\n\nThe light's color.\n\nDefault is `0xffffff`.\n\n**intensity**\n\nThe light's strength/intensity.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .isDirectionalLight : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .shadow : DirectionalLightShadow\n\nThis property holds the light's shadow configuration.\n\n### .target : Object3D\n\nThe directional light points from its position to the target's position.\n\nFor the target's position to be changed to anything other than the default, it must be added to the scene.\n\nIt is also possible to set the target to be another 3D object in the scene. The light will now track the target object.\n\n## Source\n\n[src/lights/DirectionalLight.js](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/master/src/lights/DirectionalLight.js)"
            },

                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher → Controls →*\n\n# OrbitControls\n\nOrbit controls allow the camera to orbit around a target.\n\nOrbitControls performs orbiting, dollying (zooming), and panning. Unlike [TrackballControls](TrackballControls.html), it maintains the \"up\" direction `object.up` (+Y by default).\n\n*   Orbit: Left mouse / touch: one-finger move.\n*   Zoom: Middle mouse, or mousewheel / touch: two-finger spread or squish.\n*   Pan: Right mouse, or left mouse + ctrl/meta/shiftKey, or arrow keys / touch: two-finger move.\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\nconst controls = new OrbitControls( camera, renderer.domElement );\n// controls.update() must be called after any manual changes to the camera's transform\ncamera.position.set( 0, 20, 100 );\ncontrols.update();\nfunction animate() {\n\t// required if controls.enableDamping or controls.autoRotate are set to true\n\tcontrols.update();\n\trenderer.render( scene, camera );\n}\n```\n\n## Import\n\nOrbitControls is an addon, and must be imported explicitly, see [Installation#Addons](https://threejs.org/manual/#en/installation).\n\n```js\nimport { OrbitControls } from 'three/addons/controls/OrbitControls.js';\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new OrbitControls( object : Object3D, domElement : HTMLElement )\n\nConstructs a new controls instance.\n\n**object**\n\nThe object that is managed by the controls.\n\n**domElement**\n\nThe HTML element used for event listeners.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .autoRotate : boolean\n\nSet to true to automatically rotate around the target\n\nNote that if this is enabled, you must call `update()` in your animation loop. If you want the auto-rotate speed to be independent of the frame rate (the refresh rate of the display), you must pass the time `deltaTime`, in seconds, to `update()`.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .autoRotateSpeed : number\n\nHow fast to rotate around the target if `autoRotate` is `true`. The default equates to 30 seconds per orbit at 60fps.\n\nNote that if `autoRotate` is enabled, you must call `update()` in your animation loop.\n\nDefault is `2`.\n\n### .cursor : Vector3\n\nThe focus point of the `minTargetRadius` and `maxTargetRadius` limits. It can be updated manually at any point to change the center of interest for the `target`.\n\n### .cursorStyle : 'auto' | 'grab'\n\nDefines the visual representation of the cursor.\n\nDefault is `'auto'`.\n\n### .dampingFactor : number\n\nThe damping inertia used if `enableDamping` is set to `true`.\n\nNote that for this to work, you must call `update()` in your animation loop.\n\nDefault is `0.05`.\n\n### .enableDamping : boolean\n\nSet to `true` to enable damping (inertia), which can be used to give a sense of weight to the controls. Note that if this is enabled, you must call `update()` in your animation loop.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .enablePan : boolean\n\nEnable or disable camera panning.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .enableRotate : boolean\n\nEnable or disable horizontal and vertical rotation of the camera.\n\nNote that it is possible to disable a single axis by setting the min and max of the `minPolarAngle` or `minAzimuthAngle` to the same value, which will cause the vertical or horizontal rotation to be fixed at that value.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .enableZoom : boolean\n\nEnable or disable zooming (dollying) of the camera.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .keyPanSpeed : number\n\nHow fast to pan the camera when the keyboard is used in pixels per keypress.\n\nDefault is `7`.\n\n### .keyRotateSpeed : number\n\nHow fast to rotate the camera when the keyboard is used.\n\nDefault is `1`.\n\n### .keys : Object\n\nThis object contains references to the keycodes for controlling camera panning.\n\n```js\ncontrols.keys = {\n\tLEFT: 'ArrowLeft', //left arrow\n\tUP: 'ArrowUp', // up arrow\n\tRIGHT: 'ArrowRight', // right arrow\n\tBOTTOM: 'ArrowDown' // down arrow\n}\n```\n\n**Overrides:** [Controls#keys](Controls.html#keys)\n\n### .maxAzimuthAngle : number\n\nHow far you can orbit horizontally, upper limit. If set, the interval `[ min, max ]` must be a sub-interval of `[ - 2 PI, 2 PI ]`, with `( max - min < 2 PI )`.\n\nDefault is `-Infinity`.\n\n### .minDistance : number\n\nHow far you can dolly in (perspective camera only).\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .minPolarAngle : number\n\nHow far you can orbit vertically, lower limit.                 "name": "BufferGeometry",
                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher →*\n\n# BufferGeometry\n\nA representation of mesh, line, or point geometry. Includes vertex positions, face indices, normals, colors, UVs, and custom attributes within buffers, reducing the cost of passing all this data to the GPU.\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\nconst geometry = new THREE.BufferGeometry();\n// create a simple square shape. We duplicate the top left and bottom right\n// vertices because each vertex needs to appear once per triangle.\nconst vertices = new Float32Array( [\n\t-1.0, -1.0,  1.0, // v0\n\t 1.0, -1.0,  1.0, // v1\n\t 1.0,  1.0,  1.0, // v2\n\t 1.0,  1.0,  1.0, // v3\n\t-1.0,  1.0,  1.0, // v4\n\t-1.0, -1.0,  1.0  // v5\n] );\n// itemSize = 3 because there are 3 values (components) per vertex\ngeometry.setAttribute( 'position', new THREE.BufferAttribute( vertices, 3 ) );\nconst material = new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial( { color: 0xff0000 } );\nconst mesh = new THREE.Mesh( geometry, material );\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new BufferGeometry()\n\nConstructs a new geometry.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .attributes : Object.<string, (BufferAttribute|InterleavedBufferAttribute)>\n\nThis dictionary has as id the name of the attribute to be set and as value the buffer attribute to set it to. Rather than accessing this property directly, use `setAttribute()` and `getAttribute()` to access attributes of this geometry.\n\n### .boundingBox : Box3\n\nBounding box for the geometry which can be calculated with `computeBoundingBox()`.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .boundingSphere : Sphere\n\nBounding sphere for the geometry which can be calculated with `computeBoundingSphere()`.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .drawRange : Object\n\nDetermines the part of the geometry to render. This should not be set directly, instead use `setDrawRange()`.\n\n### .groups : Array.<Object>\n\nSplit the geometry into groups, each of which will be rendered in a separate draw call. This allows an array of materials to be used with the geometry.\n\nUse `addGroup()` and `clearGroups()` to edit groups, rather than modifying this array directly.\n\nEvery vertex and index must belong to exactly one group — groups must not share vertices or indices, and must not leave vertices or indices unused.\n\n### .id : number (readonly)\n\nThe ID of the geometry.\n\n### .index : BufferAttribute\n\nAllows for vertices to be re-used across multiple triangles; this is called using \"indexed triangles\". Each triangle is associated with the indices of three vertices. This attribute therefore stores the index of each vertex for each triangular face. If this attribute is not set, the renderer assumes that each three contiguous positions represent a single triangle.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .indirect : BufferAttribute\n\nA (storage) buffer attribute which was generated with a compute shader and now defines indirect draw calls.\n\nCan only be used with [WebGPURenderer](WebGPURenderer.html) and a WebGPU backend.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .indirectOffset : number | Array.<number>\n\nThe offset, in bytes, into the indirect drawing buffer where the value data begins. If an array is provided, multiple indirect draw calls will be made for each offset.\n\nCan only be used with [WebGPURenderer](WebGPURenderer.html) and a WebGPU backend.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .isBufferGeometry : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .morphAttributes : Object\n\nThis dictionary holds the morph targets of the geometry.\n\nNote: Once the geometry has been rendered, the morph attribute data cannot be changed. You will have to call `dispose()`, and create a new geometry instance.\n\n### .morphTargetsRelative : boolean\n\nUsed to control the morph target behavior; when set to `true`, the morph target data is treated as relative offsets, rather than as absolute positions/normals.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .name : string\n\nThe name of the geometry.\n\n### .userData : Object\n\nAn object that can be used to store custom data about the geometry. It should not hold references to functions as these will not be cloned.\n\n### .uuid : string (readonly)\n\nThe UUID of the geometry.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .addGroup( start : number, count : number, materialIndex : number )\n\nAdds a group to this geometry.\n\n**start**\n\nThe first element in this draw call. That is the first vertex for non-indexed geometry, otherwise the first triangle index.\n\n**count**\n\nSpecifies how many vertices (or indices) are part of this group.\n\n**materialIndex**\n\nThe material array index to use.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .applyMatrix4( matrix : Matrix4 ) : BufferGeometry\n\nApplies the given 4x4 transformation matrix to the geometry.\n\n**matrix**\n\nThe matrix to apply.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .applyQuaternion( q : Quaternion ) : BufferGeometry\n\nApplies the rotation represented by the Quaternion to the geometry.\n\n**q**\n\nThe Quaternion to apply.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .center() : BufferGeometry\n\nCenter the geometry based on its bounding box.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .clearGroups()\n\nClears all groups.\n\n### .clone() : BufferGeometry\n\nReturns a new geometry with copied values from this instance.\n\n**Returns:** A clone of this instance.\n\n### .computeBoundingBox()\n\nComputes the bounding box of the geometry, and updates the `boundingBox` member. The bounding box is not computed by the engine; it must be computed by your app. You may need to recompute the bounding box if the geometry vertices are modified.\n\n### .computeBoundingSphere()\n\nComputes the bounding sphere of the geometry, and updates the `boundingSphere` member. The engine automatically computes the bounding sphere when it is needed, e.g., for ray casting or view frustum culling. You may need to recompute the bounding sphere if the geometry vertices are modified.\n\n### .computeTangents()\n\nCalculates and adds a tangent attribute to this geometry.\n\nThe computation is only supported for indexed geometries and if position, normal, and uv attributes are defined. When using a tangent space normal map, prefer the MikkTSpace algorithm provided by BufferGeometryUtils#computeMikkTSpaceTangents instead.\n\n### .computeVertexNormals()\n\nComputes vertex normals for the given vertex data. For indexed geometries, the method sets each vertex normal to be the average of the face normals of the faces that share that vertex. For non-indexed geometries, vertices are not shared, and the method sets each vertex normal to be the same as the face normal.\n\n### .copy( source : BufferGeometry ) : BufferGeometry\n\nCopies the values of the given geometry to this instance.\n\n**source**\n\nThe geometry to copy.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .deleteAttribute( name : string ) : BufferGeometry\n\nDeletes the attribute for the given name.\n\n**name**\n\nThe attribute name to delete.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .dispose()\n\nFrees the GPU-related resources allocated by this instance. Call this method whenever this instance is no longer used in your app.\n\n##### Fires:\n\n*   BufferGeometry#event:dispose\n\n### .getAttribute( name : string ) : BufferAttribute | InterleavedBufferAttribute | undefined\n\nReturns the buffer attribute for the given name.\n\n**name**\n\nThe attribute name.\n\n**Returns:** The buffer attribute. Returns `undefined` if not attribute has been found.\n\n### .getIndex() : BufferAttribute\n\nReturns the index of this geometry.\n\n**Returns:** The index. Returns `null` if no index is defined.\n\n### .getIndirect() : BufferAttribute\n\nReturns the indirect attribute of this geometry.\n\n**Returns:** The indirect attribute. Returns `null` if no indirect attribute is defined.\n\n### .hasAttribute( name : string ) : boolean\n\nReturns `true` if this geometry has an attribute for the given name.\n\n**name**\n\nThe attribute name.\n\n**Returns:** Whether this geometry has an attribute for the given name or not.\n\n### .lookAt( vector : Vector3 ) : BufferGeometry\n\nRotates the geometry to face a point in 3D space. This is typically done as a one time operation, and not during a loop. Use [Object3D#lookAt](Object3D.htm"

                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher →*\n\n# Object3D\n\nThis is the base class for most objects in three.js and provides a set of properties and methods for manipulating objects in 3D space.\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new Object3D()\n\nConstructs a new 3D object.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .animations : Array.<AnimationClip>\n\nAn array holding the animation clips of the 3D object.\n\n### .castShadow : boolean\n\nWhen set to `true`, the 3D object gets rendered into shadow maps.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .children : Array.<Object3D>\n\nAn array holding the child 3D objects of this instance.\n\n### .customDepthMaterial : Material | undefined\n\nCustom depth material to be used when rendering to the depth map. Can only be used in context of meshes. When shadow-casting with a [DirectionalLight](DirectionalLight.html) or [SpotLight](SpotLight.html), if you are modifying vertex positions in the vertex shader you must specify a custom depth material for proper shadows.\n\nOnly relevant in context of [WebGLRenderer](WebGLRenderer.html).\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n### .customDistanceMaterial : Material | undefined\n\nSame as [Object3D#customDepthMaterial](Object3D.html#customDepthMaterial), but used with [PointLight](PointLight.html).\n\nOnly relevant in context of [WebGLRenderer](WebGLRenderer.html).\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n### .frustumCulled : boolean\n\nWhen set to `true`, the 3D object is honored by view frustum culling.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .id : number (readonly)\n\nThe ID of the 3D object.\n\n### .isObject3D : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .layers : Layers\n\nThe layer membership of the 3D object. The 3D object is only visible if it has at least one layer in common with the camera in use. This property can also be used to filter out unwanted objects in ray-intersection tests when using [Raycaster](Raycaster.html).\n\n### .matrix : Matrix4\n\nRepresents the object's transformation matrix in local space.\n\n### .matrixAutoUpdate : boolean\n\nWhen set to `true`, the engine automatically computes the local matrix from position, rotation and scale every frame. If set to `false`, the app is responsible for recomputing the local matrix by calling `updateMatrix()`.\n\nThe default values for all 3D objects is defined by `Object3D.DEFAULT_MATRIX_AUTO_UPDATE`.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .matrixWorld : Matrix4\n\nRepresents the object's transformation matrix in world space. If the 3D object has no parent, then it's identical to the local transformation matrix\n\n### .matrixWorldAutoUpdate : boolean\n\nWhen set to `true`, the engine automatically computes the world matrix from the current local matrix and the object's transformation hierarchy. If set to `false`, the app is responsible for recomputing the world matrix by directly updating the `matrixWorld` property.\n\nThe default values for all 3D objects is defined by `Object3D.DEFAULT_MATRIX_WORLD_AUTO_UPDATE`.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .matrixWorldNeedsUpdate : boolean\n\nWhen set to `true`, it calculates the world matrix in that frame and resets this property to `false`.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .modelViewMatrix : Matrix4\n\nRepresents the object's model-view matrix.\n\n### .name : string\n\nThe name of the 3D object.\n\n### .normalMatrix : Matrix3\n\nRepresents the object's normal matrix.\n\n### .parent : Object3D\n\nA reference to the parent object.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .pivot : Vector3\n\nThe pivot point for rotation and scale transformations. When set, rotation and scale are applied around this point instead of the object's origin.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .position : Vector3\n\nRepresents the object's local position.\n\nDefault is `(0,0,0)`.\n\n### .quaternion : Quaternion\n\nRepresents the object's local rotation as Quaternions.\n\n### .receiveShadow : boolean\n\nWhen set to `true`, the 3D object is affected by shadows in the scene.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .renderOrder : number\n\nThis value allows the default rendering order of scene graph objects to be overridden although opaque and transparent objects remain sorted independently. When this property is set for an instance of [Group](Group.html),all descendants objects will be sorted and rendered together. Sorting is from lowest to highest render order.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .rotation : Euler\n\nRepresents the object's local rotation as Euler angles, in radians.\n\nDefault is `(0,0,0)`.\n\n### .scale : Vector3\n\nRepresents the object's local scale.\n\nDefault is `(1,1,1)`.\n\n### .static : boolean\n\nWhether the 3D object is supposed to be static or not. If set to `true`, it means the 3D object is not going to be changed after the initial renderer. This includes geometry and material settings. A static 3D object can be processed by the renderer slightly faster since certain state checks can be bypassed.\n\nOnly relevant in context of [WebGPURenderer](WebGPURenderer.html).\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .type : string (readonly)\n\nThe type property is used for detecting the object type in context of serialization/deserialization.\n\n### .up : Vector3\n\nDefines the `up` direction of the 3D object which influences the orientation via methods like [Object3D#lookAt](Object3D.html#lookAt).\n\nThe default values for all 3D objects is defined by `Object3D.DEFAULT_UP`.\n\n### .userData : Object\n\nAn object that can be used to store custom data about the 3D object. It should not hold references to functions as these will not be cloned.\n\n### .uuid : string (readonly)\n\nThe UUID of the 3D object.\n\n### .visible : boolean\n\nWhen set to `true`, the 3D object gets rendered.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .DEFAULT_MATRIX_AUTO_UPDATE : boolean\n\nThe default setting for [Object3D#matrixAutoUpdate](Object3D.html#matrixAutoUpdate) for newly created 3D objects.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .DEFAULT_MATRIX_WORLD_AUTO_UPDATE : boolean\n\nThe default setting for [Object3D#matrixWorldAutoUpdate](Object3D.html#matrixWorldAutoUpdate) for newly created 3D objects.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .DEFAULT_UP : Vector3\n\nThe default up direction for objects, also used as the default position for [DirectionalLight](DirectionalLight.html) and [HemisphereLight](HemisphereLight.html).\n\nDefault is `(0,1,0)`.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .add( object : Object3D ) : Object3D\n\nAdds the given 3D object as a child to this 3D object. An arbitrary number of objects may be added. Any current parent on an object passed in here will be removed, since an object can have at most one parent.\n\n**object**\n\nThe 3D object to add.\n\n##### Fires:\n\n*   [Object3D#event:added](Object3D.html#event:added)\n*   [Object3D#event:childadded](Object3D.html#event:childadded)\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .applyMatrix4( matrix : Matrix4 )\n\nApplies the given transformation matrix to the object and updates the object's position, rotation and scale.\n\n**matrix**\n\nThe transformation matrix.\n\n### .applyQuaternion( q : Quaternion ) : Object3D\n\nApplies a rotation represented by given the quaternion to the 3D object.\n\n**q**\n\nThe quaternion.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .attach( object : Object3D ) : Object3D\n\nAdds the given 3D object as a child of this 3D object, while maintaining the object's world transform. This method does not support scene graphs having non-uniformly-scaled nodes(s).\n\n**object**\n\nThe 3D object to attach.\n\n##### Fires:\n\n*   [Object3D#event:added](Object3D.html#event:added)\n*   [Object3D#event:childadded](Object3D.html#event:childadded)\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .clear() : Object3D\n\nRemoves all child objects.\n\n##### Fires:\n\n*   [Object3D#event:removed](Object3D.html#event:removed)\n*   [Object3D#event:childremoved](Object3D.html#event:childremoved)\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this instance.\n\n### .clone( recursive : boolean ) : Object3D\n\nReturns a new 3D object with copied values from this instance.\n\n**recursive**\n\nWhen set to `true`, descendants of the 3D object are also cloned.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n**Returns:** A clone of this instance.\n\n### .copy( source : Object3D, recursive : boolean ) : Object3D\n\nCopies the values of the given 3D object to th"

                "content": "*Inheritance: EventDispatcher →*\n\n# Texture\n\nBase class for all textures.\n\nNote: After the initial use of a texture, its dimensions, format, and type cannot be changed. Instead, call [Texture#dispose](Texture.html#dispose) on the texture and instantiate a new one.\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new Texture( image : Object, mapping : number, wrapS : number, wrapT : number, magFilter : number, minFilter : number, format : number, type : number, anisotropy : number, colorSpace : string )\n\nConstructs a new texture.\n\n**image**\n\nThe image holding the texture data.\n\nDefault is `Texture.DEFAULT_IMAGE`.\n\n**mapping**\n\nThe texture mapping.\n\nDefault is `Texture.DEFAULT_MAPPING`.\n\n**wrapS**\n\nThe wrapS value.\n\nDefault is `ClampToEdgeWrapping`.\n\n**wrapT**\n\nThe wrapT value.\n\nDefault is `ClampToEdgeWrapping`.\n\n**magFilter**\n\nThe mag filter value.\n\nDefault is `LinearFilter`.\n\n**minFilter**\n\nThe min filter value.\n\nDefault is `LinearMipmapLinearFilter`.\n\n**format**\n\nThe texture format.\n\nDefault is `RGBAFormat`.\n\n**type**\n\nThe texture type.\n\nDefault is `UnsignedByteType`.\n\n**anisotropy**\n\nThe anisotropy value.\n\nDefault is `Texture.DEFAULT_ANISOTROPY`.\n\n**colorSpace**\n\nThe color space.\n\nDefault is `NoColorSpace`.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .anisotropy : number\n\nThe number of samples taken along the axis through the pixel that has the highest density of texels. By default, this value is `1`. A higher value gives a less blurry result than a basic mipmap, at the cost of more texture samples being used.\n\nDefault is `Texture.DEFAULT_ANISOTROPY`.\n\n### .center : Vector2\n\nThe point around which rotation occurs. A value of `(0.5, 0.5)` corresponds to the center of the texture. Default is `(0, 0)`, the lower left.\n\nDefault is `(0,0)`.\n\n### .channel : number\n\nLets you select the uv attribute to map the texture to. `0` for `uv`, `1` for `uv1`, `2` for `uv2` and `3` for `uv3`.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .colorSpace : string\n\nTextures containing color data should be annotated with `SRGBColorSpace` or `LinearSRGBColorSpace`.\n\nDefault is `NoColorSpace`.\n\n### .depth\n\nThe depth of the texture in pixels.\n\n### .flipY : boolean\n\nIf set to `true`, the texture is flipped along the vertical axis when uploaded to the GPU.\n\nNote that this property has no effect when using `ImageBitmap`. You need to configure the flip on bitmap creation instead.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .format : number\n\nThe format of the texture.\n\nDefault is `RGBAFormat`.\n\n### .generateMipmaps : boolean\n\nWhether to generate mipmaps (if possible) for a texture.\n\nSet this to `false` if you are creating mipmaps manually.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .height\n\nThe height of the texture in pixels.\n\n### .id : number (readonly)\n\nThe ID of the texture.\n\n### .image : Object\n\nThe image object holding the texture data.\n\n### .internalFormat : string\n\nThe default internal format is derived from [Texture#format](Texture.html#format) and [Texture#type](Texture.html#type) and defines how the texture data is going to be stored on the GPU.\n\nThis property allows to overwrite the default format.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .isArrayTexture : boolean (readonly)\n\nIndicates if a texture should be handled like a texture array.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .isRenderTargetTexture : boolean (readonly)\n\nIndicates whether a texture belongs to a render target or not.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .isTexture : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .magFilter : NearestFilter | NearestMipmapNearestFilter | NearestMipmapLinearFilter | LinearFilter | LinearMipmapNearestFilter | LinearMipmapLinearFilter\n\nHow the texture is sampled when a texel covers more than one pixel.\n\nDefault is `LinearFilter`.\n\n### .mapping : UVMapping | CubeReflectionMapping | CubeRefractionMapping | EquirectangularReflectionMapping | EquirectangularRefractionMapping | CubeUVReflectionMapping\n\nHow the texture is applied to the object. The value `UVMapping` is the default, where texture or uv coordinates are used to apply the map.\n\nDefault is `UVMapping`.\n\n### .matrix : Matrix3\n\nThe uv-transformation matrix of the texture.\n\n### .matrixAutoUpdate : boolean\n\nWhether to update the texture's uv-transformation [Texture#matrix](Texture.html#matrix) from the properties [Texture#offset](Texture.html#offset), [Texture#repeat](Texture.html#repeat), [Texture#rotation](Texture.html#rotation), and [Texture#center](Texture.html#center).\n\nSet this to `false` if you are specifying the uv-transform matrix directly.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .minFilter : NearestFilter | NearestMipmapNearestFilter | NearestMipmapLinearFilter | LinearFilter | LinearMipmapNearestFilter | LinearMipmapLinearFilter\n\nHow the texture is sampled when a texel covers less than one pixel.\n\nDefault is `LinearMipmapLinearFilter`.\n\n### .mipmaps : Array.<Object>\n\nAn array holding user-defined mipmaps.\n\n### .name : string\n\nThe name of the texture.\n\n### .needsPMREMUpdate : boolean\n\nSetting this property to `true` indicates the engine the PMREM must be regenerated.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .needsUpdate : boolean\n\nSetting this property to `true` indicates the engine the texture must be updated in the next render. This triggers a texture upload to the GPU and ensures correct texture parameter configuration.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .offset : Vector2\n\nHow much a single repetition of the texture is offset from the beginning, in each direction U and V. Typical range is `0.0` to `1.0`.\n\nDefault is `(0,0)`.\n\n### .onUpdate : function\n\nA callback function, called when the texture is updated (e.g., when [Texture#needsUpdate](Texture.html#needsUpdate) has been set to true and then the texture is used).\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .pmremVersion : number (readonly)\n\nIndicates whether this texture should be processed by `PMREMGenerator` or not (only relevant for render target textures).\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .premultiplyAlpha : boolean\n\nIf set to `true`, the alpha channel, if present, is multiplied into the color channels when the texture is uploaded to the GPU.\n\nNote that this property has no effect when using `ImageBitmap`. You need to configure premultiply alpha on bitmap creation instead.\n\nDefault is `false`.\n\n### .renderTarget : RenderTarget | WebGLRenderTarget\n\nAn optional back reference to the textures render target.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .repeat : Vector2\n\nHow many times the texture is repeated across the surface, in each direction U and V. If repeat is set greater than `1` in either direction, the corresponding wrap parameter should also be set to `RepeatWrapping` or `MirroredRepeatWrapping` to achieve the desired tiling effect.\n\nDefault is `(1,1)`.\n\n### .rotation : number\n\nHow much the texture is rotated around the center point, in radians. Positive values are counter-clockwise.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .source : Source\n\nThe data definition of a texture. A reference to the data source can be shared across textures. This is often useful in context of spritesheets where multiple textures render the same data but with different texture transformations.\n\n### .type : number\n\nThe data type of the texture.\n\nDefault is `UnsignedByteType`.\n\n### .unpackAlignment : number\n\nSpecifies the alignment requirements for the start of each pixel row in memory. The allowable values are `1` (byte-alignment), `2` (rows aligned to even-numbered bytes), `4` (word-alignment), and `8` (rows start on double-word boundaries).\n\nDefault is `4`.\n\n### .updateRanges : Array.<Object>\n\nThis can be used to only update a subregion or specific rows of the texture (for example, just the first 3 rows). Use the `addUpdateRange()` function to add ranges to this array.\n\n### .userData : Object\n\nAn object that can be used to store custom data about the texture. It should not hold references to functions as these will not be cloned.\n\n### .uuid : string (readonly)\n\nThe UUID of the texture.\n\n### .version : number (readonly)\n\nThis starts at `0` and counts how many times [Texture#needsUpdate](Texture.html#needsUpdate) is set to `true`.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .width\n\nThe width of the texture in"

Images are internally loaded via [ImageLoader](ImageLoader.html).\n\nPlease note that `TextureLoader` has dropped support for progress events in `r84`. The method also returns a new texture object which can directly be used for material creation. If you do it this way, the texture may pop up in your scene once the respective loading process is finished.\n\n**url**\n\nThe path/URL of the file to be loaded. This can also be a data URI.\n\n**onLoad**\n\nExecuted when the loading process has been finished.\n\n**onProgress**\n\nUnsupported in this loader.\n\n**onError**\n\nExecuted when errors occur.\n\n**Overrides:** [Loader#load](Loader.html#load)\n\n**Returns:** The texture.\n\n## Source\n\n[src/loaders/TextureLoader.js](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/master/src/loaders/TextureLoader.js)"

                "name": "Vector3",
                "content": "# Vector3\n\nClass representing a 3D vector. A 3D vector is an ordered triplet of numbers (labeled x, y and z), which can be used to represent a number of things, such as:\n\n*   A point in 3D space.\n*   A direction and length in 3D space. In three.js the length will always be the Euclidean distance(straight-line distance) from `(0, 0, 0)` to `(x, y, z)` and the direction is also measured from `(0, 0, 0)` towards `(x, y, z)`.\n*   Any arbitrary ordered triplet of numbers.\n\nThere are other things a 3D vector can be used to represent, such as momentum vectors and so on, however these are the most common uses in three.js.\n\nIterating through a vector instance will yield its components `(x, y, z)` in the corresponding order.\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\nconst a = new THREE.Vector3( 0, 1, 0 );\n//no arguments; will be initialised to (0, 0, 0)\nconst b = new THREE.Vector3( );\nconst d = a.distanceTo( b );\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new Vector3( x : number, y : number, z : number )\n\nConstructs a new 3D vector.\n\n**x**\n\nThe x value of this vector.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n**y**\n\nThe y value of this vector.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n**z**\n\nThe z value of this vector.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .isVector3 : boolean (readonly)\n\nThis flag can be used for type testing.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n### .x : number\n\nThe x value of this vector.\n\n### .y : number\n\nThe y value of this vector.\n\n### .z : number\n\nThe z value of this vector.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .add( v : Vector3 ) : Vector3\n\nAdds the given vector to this instance.\n\n**v**\n\nThe vector to add.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .addScalar( s : number ) : Vector3\n\nAdds the given scalar value to all components of this instance.\n\n**s**\n\nThe scalar to add.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .addScaledVector( v : Vector3 | Vector4, s : number ) : Vector3\n\nAdds the given vector scaled by the given factor to this instance.\n\n**v**\n\nThe vector.\n\n**s**\n\nThe factor that scales `v`.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .addVectors( a : Vector3, b : Vector3 ) : Vector3\n\nAdds the given vectors and stores the result in this instance.\n\n**a**\n\nThe first vector.\n\n**b**\n\nThe second vector.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .angleTo( v : Vector3 ) : number\n\nReturns the angle between the given vector and this instance in radians.\n\n**v**\n\nThe vector to compute the angle with.\n\n**Returns:** The angle in radians.\n\n### .applyAxisAngle( axis : Vector3, angle : number ) : Vector3\n\nApplies a rotation specified by an axis and an angle to this vector.\n\n**axis**\n\nA normalized vector representing the rotation axis.\n\n**angle**\n\nThe angle in radians.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .applyEuler( euler : Euler ) : Vector3\n\nApplies the given Euler rotation to this vector.\n\n**euler**\n\nThe Euler angles.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .applyMatrix3( m : Matrix3 ) : Vector3\n\nMultiplies this vector with the given 3x3 matrix.\n\n**m**\n\nThe 3x3 matrix.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .applyMatrix4( m : Matrix4 ) : Vector3\n\nMultiplies this vector (with an implicit 1 in the 4th dimension) by m, and divides by perspective.\n\n**m**\n\nThe matrix to apply.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .applyNormalMatrix( m : Matrix3 ) : Vector3\n\nMultiplies this vector by the given normal matrix and normalizes the result.\n\n**m**\n\nThe normal matrix.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .applyQuaternion( q : Quaternion ) : Vector3\n\nApplies the given Quaternion to this vector.\n\n**q**\n\nThe Quaternion.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .ceil() : Vector3\n\nThe components of this vector are rounded up to the nearest integer value.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .clamp( min : Vector3, max : Vector3 ) : Vector3\n\nIf this vector's x, y or z value is greater than the max vector's x, y or z value, it is replaced by the corresponding value. If this vector's x, y or z value is less than the min vector's x, y or z value, it is replaced by the corresponding value.\n\n**min**\n\nThe minimum x, y and z values.\n\n**max**\n\nThe maximum x, y and z values in the desired range.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .clampLength( min : number, max : number ) : Vector3\n\nIf this vector's length is greater than the max value, it is replaced by the max value. If this vector's length is less than the min value, it is replaced by the min value.\n\n**min**\n\nThe minimum value the vector length will be clamped to.\n\n**max**\n\nThe maximum value the vector length will be clamped to.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .clampScalar( minVal : number, maxVal : number ) : Vector3\n\nIf this vector's x, y or z values are greater than the max value, they are replaced by the max value. If this vector's x, y or z values are less than the min value, they are replaced by the min value.\n\n**minVal**\n\nThe minimum value the components will be clamped to.\n\n**maxVal**\n\nThe maximum value the components will be clamped to.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .clone() : Vector3\n\nReturns a new vector with copied values from this instance.\n\n**Returns:** A clone of this instance.\n\n### .copy( v : Vector3 ) : Vector3\n\nCopies the values of the given vector to this instance.\n\n**v**\n\nThe vector to copy.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .cross( v : Vector3 ) : Vector3\n\nCalculates the cross product of the given vector with this instance.\n\n**v**\n\nThe vector to compute the cross product with.\n\n**Returns:** The result of the cross product.\n\n### .crossVectors( a : Vector3, b : Vector3 ) : Vector3\n\nCalculates the cross product of the given vectors and stores the result in this instance.\n\n**a**\n\nThe first vector.\n\n**b**\n\nThe second vector.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this vector.\n\n### .distanceTo( v : Vector3 ) : number\n\nComputes the distance from the given vector to this instance.\n\n**v**\n\nThe vector to compute the distance to.\n\n**Returns:** The distance.\n\n### .distanceToSquared( v : Vector3 ) : number\n\nComputes the squared distance from the given vector to this instance. If you are just comparing the distance with another distance, you should compare the distance squared instead as it is slightly more efficient to calculate.\n\n**v**\n\nThe vector to compute the                 "content": "# Color\n\nA Color instance is represented by RGB components in the linear _working color space_, which defaults to `LinearSRGBColorSpace`. Inputs conventionally using `SRGBColorSpace` (such as hexadecimals and CSS strings) are converted to the working color space automatically.\n\nSource color spaces may be specified explicitly, to ensure correct conversions.\n\n```js\n// assumed already LinearSRGBColorSpace; no conversion\nconst color = new THREE.Color().setRGB( 0.5, 0.5, 0.5 );\n// converted explicitly from SRGBColorSpace to LinearSRGBColorSpace\nconst color = new THREE.Color().setRGB( 0.5, 0.5, 0.5, SRGBColorSpace );\n```\n\nIf THREE.ColorManagement is disabled, no conversions occur. For details, see _Color management_. Iterating through a Color instance will yield its components (r, g, b) in the corresponding order. A Color can be initialised in any of the following ways:\n\n```js\n//empty constructor - will default white\nconst color1 = new THREE.Color();\n//Hexadecimal color (recommended)\nconst color2 = new THREE.Color( 0xff0000 );\n//RGB string\nconst color3 = new THREE.Color(\"rgb(255, 0, 0)\");\nconst color4 = new THREE.Color(\"rgb(100%, 0%, 0%)\");\n//X11 color name - all 140 color names are supported.\n//Note the lack of CamelCase in the name\nconst color5 = new THREE.Color( 'skyblue' );\n//HSL string\nconst color6 = new THREE.Color(\"hsl(0, 100%, 50%)\");\n//Separate RGB values between 0 and 1\nconst color7 = new THREE.Color( 1, 0, 0 );\n```\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\n// converted automatically from SRGBColorSpace to LinearSRGBColorSpace\nconst color = new THREE.Color().setHex( 0x112233 );\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new Color( r : number | string | Color, g : number, b : number )\n\nConstructs a new color.\n\nNote that standard method of specifying color in three.js is with a hexadecimal triplet, and that method is used throughout the rest of the documentation.\n\n**r**\n\nThe red component of the color.                 "name": "Raycaster",
                "content": "# Raycaster\n\nThis class is designed to assist with raycasting. Raycasting is used for mouse picking (working out what objects in the 3d space the mouse is over) amongst other things.\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new Raycaster( origin : Vector3, direction : Vector3, near : number, far : number )\n\nConstructs a new raycaster.\n\n**origin**\n\nThe origin vector where the ray casts from.\n\n**direction**\n\nThe (normalized) direction vector that gives direction to the ray.\n\n**near**\n\nAll results returned are further away than near. Near can't be negative.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n**far**\n\nAll results returned are closer than far. Far can't be lower than near.\n\nDefault is `Infinity`.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .camera : Camera\n\nThe camera to use when raycasting against view-dependent objects such as billboarded objects like sprites. This field can be set manually or is set when calling `setFromCamera()`.\n\nDefault is `null`.\n\n### .far : number\n\nAll results returned are closer than far. Far can't be lower than near.\n\nDefault is `Infinity`.\n\n### .layers : Layers\n\nAllows to selectively ignore 3D objects when performing intersection tests. The following code example ensures that only 3D objects on layer `1` will be honored by raycaster.\n\n```js\nraycaster.layers.set( 1 );\nobject.layers.enable( 1 );\n```\n\n### .near : number\n\nAll results returned are further away than near. Near can't be negative.\n\nDefault is `0`.\n\n### .params : Object\n\nA parameter object that configures the raycasting. It has the structure:\n\n```js\n{\n\tMesh: {},\n\tLine: { threshold: 1 },\n\tLOD: {},\n\tPoints: { threshold: 1 },\n\tSprite: {}\n}\n```\n\nWhere `threshold` is the precision of the raycaster when intersecting objects, in world units.\n\n### .ray : Ray\n\nThe ray used for raycasting.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .intersectObject( object : Object3D, recursive : boolean, intersects : Array.<Raycaster~Intersection> ) : Array.<Raycaster~Intersection>\n\nChecks all intersection between the ray and the object with or without the descendants. Intersections are returned sorted by distance, closest first.\n\n`Raycaster` delegates to the `raycast()` method of the passed 3D object, when evaluating whether the ray intersects the object or not. This allows meshes to respond differently to ray casting than lines or points.\n\nNote that for meshes, faces must be pointed towards the origin of the ray in order to be detected; intersections of the ray passing through the back of a face will not be detected. To raycast against both faces of an object, you'll want to set [Material#side](Material.html#side) to `THREE.DoubleSide`.\n\n**object**\n\nThe 3D object to check for intersection with the ray.\n\n**recursive**\n\nIf set to `true`, it also checks all descendants. Otherwise it only checks intersection with the object.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n**intersects**\n\nThe target array that holds the result of the method.\n\nDefault is `[]`.\n\n**Returns:** An array holding the intersection points.\n\n### .intersectObjects( objects : Array.<Object3D>, recursive : boolean, intersects : Array.<Raycaster~Intersection> ) : Array.<Raycaster~Intersection>\n\nChecks all intersection between the ray and the objects with or without the descendants. Intersections are returned sorted by distance, closest first.\n\n**objects**\n\nThe 3D objects to check for intersection with the ray.\n\n**recursive**\n\nIf set to `true`, it also checks all descendants. Otherwise it only checks intersection with the object.\n\nDefault is `true`.\n\n**intersects**\n\nThe target array that holds the result of the method.\n\nDefault is `[]`.\n\n**Returns:** An array holding the intersection points.\n\n### .set( origin : Vector3, direction : Vector3 )\n\nUpdates the ray with a new origin and direction by copying the values from the arguments.\n\n**origin**\n\nThe origin vector where the ray casts from.\n\n**direction**\n\nThe (normalized) direction vector that gives direction to the ray.\n\n### .setFromCamera( coords : Vector2, camera : Camera )\n\nUses the given coordinates and camera to compute a new origin and direction for the internal ray.\n\n**coords**\n\n2D coordinates of the mouse, in normalized device coordinates (NDC). X and Y components should be between `-1` and `1`.\n\n**camera**\n\nThe camera from which the ray should originate.\n\n### .setFromXRController( controller : WebXRController ) : Raycaster\n\nUses the given WebXR controller to compute a new origin and direction for the internal ray.\n\n**controller**\n\nThe controller to copy the position and direction from.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this raycaster.\n\n## Type Definitions\n\n### .Intersection\n\nThe intersection point of a raycaster intersection test.\n\n**distance**  \nnumber\n\nThe distance from the ray's origin to the intersection point.\n\n**distanceToRay**  \nnumber\n\nSome 3D objects e.g. [Points](Points.html) provide the distance of the intersection to the nearest point on the ray. For other objects it will be `undefined`.\n\n**point**  \n[Vector3](Vector3.html)\n\nThe intersection point, in world coordinates.\n\n**face**  \nObject\n\nThe face that has been intersected.\n\n**faceIndex**  \nnumber\n\nThe face index.\n\n**object**  \n[Object3D](Object3D.html)\n\nThe 3D object that has been intersected.\n\n**uv**  \n[Vector2](Vector2.html)\n\nU,V coordinates at point of intersection.\n\n**uv1**  \n[Vector2](Vector2.html)\n\nSecond set of U,V coordinates at point of intersection.\n\n**normal**  \n[Vector3](Vector3.html)\n\nInterpolated normal vector at point of intersection.\n\n**instanceId**  \nnumber\n\nThe index number of the instance where the ray intersects the [InstancedMesh](InstancedMesh.html).\n\n## Source\n\n[src/core/Raycaster.js](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/master/src/core/Raycaster.js)"

                "content": "# LoadingManager\n\nHandles and keeps track of loaded and pending data. A default global instance of this class is created and used by loaders if not supplied manually.\n\nIn general that should be sufficient, however there are times when it can be useful to have separate loaders - for example if you want to show separate loading bars for objects and textures.\n\n## Code Example\n\n```js\nconst manager = new THREE.LoadingManager();\nmanager.onLoad = () => console.log( 'Loading complete!' );\nconst loader1 = new OBJLoader( manager );\nconst loader2 = new ColladaLoader( manager );\n```\n\n## Constructor\n\n### new LoadingManager( onLoad : function, onProgress : function, onError : function )\n\nConstructs a new loading manager.\n\n**onLoad**\n\nExecutes when all items have been loaded.\n\n**onProgress**\n\nExecutes when single items have been loaded.\n\n**onError**\n\nExecutes when an error occurs.\n\n## Properties\n\n### .abortController : AbortController\n\nUsed for aborting ongoing requests in loaders using this manager.\n\n### .onError : function | undefined\n\nExecutes when an error occurs.\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n### .onLoad : function | undefined\n\nExecutes when all items have been loaded.\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n### .onProgress : function | undefined\n\nExecutes when single items have been loaded.\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n### .onStart : function | undefined\n\nExecutes when an item starts loading.\n\nDefault is `undefined`.\n\n## Methods\n\n### .abort() : LoadingManager\n\nCan be used to abort ongoing loading requests in loaders using this manager. The abort only works if the loaders implement [Loader#abort](Loader.html#abort) and `AbortSignal.any()` is supported in the browser.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this loading manager.\n\n### .addHandler( regex : string, loader : Loader ) : LoadingManager\n\nRegisters a loader with the given regular expression. Can be used to define what loader should be used in order to load specific files. A typical use case is to overwrite the default loader for textures.\n\n```js\n// add handler for TGA textures\nmanager.addHandler( /\\.tga$/i, new TGALoader() );\n```\n\n**regex**\n\nA regular expression.\n\n**loader**\n\nA loader that should handle matched cases.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this loading manager.\n\n### .getHandler( file : string ) : Loader\n\nCan be used to retrieve the registered loader for the given file path.\n\n**file**\n\nThe file path.\n\n**Returns:** The registered loader. Returns `null` if no loader was found.\n\n### .itemEnd( url : string )\n\nThis should be called by any loader using the manager when the loader ended loading an item.\n\n**url**\n\nThe URL of the loaded item.\n\n### .itemError( url : string )\n\nThis should be called by any loader using the manager when the loader encounters an error when loading an item.\n\n**url**\n\nThe URL of the item that produces an error.\n\n### .itemStart( url : string )\n\nThis should be called by any loader using the manager when the loader starts loading an item.\n\n**url**\n\nThe URL to load.\n\n### .removeHandler( regex : string ) : LoadingManager\n\nRemoves the loader for the given regular expression.\n\n**regex**\n\nA regular expression.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this loading manager.\n\n### .resolveURL( url : string ) : string\n\nGiven a URL, uses the URL modifier callback (if any) and returns a resolved URL. If no URL modifier is set, returns the original URL.\n\n**url**\n\nThe URL to load.\n\n**Returns:** The resolved URL.\n\n### .setURLModifier( transform : function ) : LoadingManager\n\nIf provided, the callback will be passed each resource URL before a request is sent. The callback may return the original URL, or a new URL to override loading behavior. This behavior can be used to load assets from .ZIP files, drag-and-drop APIs, and Data URIs.\n\n```js\nconst blobs = {'fish.gltf': blob1, 'diffuse.png': blob2, 'normal.png': blob3};\nconst manager = new THREE.LoadingManager();\n// Initialize loading manager with URL callback.\nconst objectURLs = [];\nmanager.setURLModifier( ( url ) => {\n\turl = URL.createObjectURL( blobs[ url ] );\n\tobjectURLs.push( url );\n\treturn url;\n} );\n// Load as usual, then revoke the blob URLs.\nconst loader = new GLTFLoader( manager );\nloader.load( 'fish.gltf', (gltf) => {\n\tscene.add( gltf.scene );\n\tobjectURLs.forEach( ( url ) => URL.revokeObjectURL( url ) );\n} );\n```\n\n**transform**\n\nURL modifier callback. Called with an URL and must return a resolved URL.\n\n**Returns:** A reference to this loading manager.\n\n## Source\n\n[src/loaders/LoadingManager.js](https://github.com/mrdoob/three.js/blob/master/src/loaders/LoadingManager.js)"
